# Crusader Kings III — 1.20.0.2 "Crozier" → 1.21.0 "Florin"

## Modder's Change Report & Migration Guide

**1.21 "Florin" was released on 2026-11-17 alongside *Silk & Silver*.** This report compares
`base/1.20.0.2` directly with the launch snapshot `base/1.21.0.0`. It includes the intervening
1.20 hotfixes. Here, “1.21” means that specific launch build, not subsequent hotfixes.

The largest changes are the economy, districts, trade, playable republics and merchants, and roads.
**A mod that works on 1.20.0.2 needs a compatibility review even if it does not use the new DLC.**
Development, county opinion, control growth, building scopes and several government fields changed in
the free update. The Faith → Rite conversion already happened in 1.20; see
[1.20.0.2.md](1.20.0.2.md) for that earlier migration.

This is a comparison of shipped files and documentation. Risk ratings describe likely failure points;
they have not been confirmed by launching a mod on 1.21. The appendices contain complete removed-key
lists for the databases examined, a database change index, and commands for inspecting every file diff.

## 2. Crash & Breakage Risk Chart

### 🔴 High Risk

These are structural incompatibilities that can prevent setup or break core systems. “High risk” does
not mean that a crash was reproduced.

| # | What your mod does | What changed | Migration |
|---|---|---|---|
| H1 | Replaces `common/holdings`, old building files, or large parts of province history | Holdings list districts; buildings belong to districts. The old province-wide building layout no longer describes the system | Convert definitions, history and effects together; see [§7](#7-holdings-districts--buildings) |
| H2 | Uses province-scoped `add_building`, `remove_building`, or free-building-slot checks | Building operations/checks now need the appropriate district scope | Select/create the district, then operate on its buildings. Keep province-scoped construction callbacks in their documented scope |
| H3 | Replaces `gui/window_county_view.gui`, holding widgets, or the old succession-law window | The county view is rebuilt around districts, needs and settlement size; `window_succession_change_law.gui` is deleted | Rebase UI work on the new views and `window_change_law.gui`; updating text alone is insufficient |
| H4 | Ships a custom map or old map files | Both province layout data and map images changed; trade also requires roads and their spline assets | Merge map components as a consistent set and build a matching road network; see [§10](#10-roads--map-changes) |
| H5 | Defines an administrative government while relying on implicit title-MAA settings | `title_maa_setup` has no default; the documented mode is `main_tier_and_top_liege` | Set `title_maa_setup` and the appropriate `minimum_provincial_maa_tier` explicitly |

### 🟠 Medium Risk

| # | What your mod does | Likely failure | Migration |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1 | Uses old Development effects, triggers, modifiers, GUI calls or defines | Unknown names or effects that no longer feed the new economy | Migrate to Prosperity, then review the values and meaning; [§4](#4-development--prosperity) |
| M2 | Adds county opinion or monthly control growth | Missing modifiers, failed triggers or incorrect control balance | Use popular opinion and control-baseline/convergence mechanics; [§5](#5-popular-opinion-control--settlement-size) |
| M3 | Uses old tax modifiers or province `monthly_income` | Wrong income category or wrong scope; tax bonuses may disappear | Audit character income separately from province tax; [§6](#6-tax-categories--income) |
| M4 | Calls one of the five changed scripted effects | Missing substitutions or changed transfer behavior | Update the arguments in [§15](#15-changed-scripted-effect-parameters) |
| M5 | Overrides `is_governor`, `governor_efficiency` or old house-power calculations | Former script definitions moved into engine-backed calculations | Use the new efficiency modifiers and government `house_power`; [§14](#14-script-api-changes--new-engine-features) |
| M6 | Replaces law, confederation or government files | Old succession approval, confederation membership or modifier placement no longer matches vanilla | Merge assembly rules, `confederation_base` and cohesion-level modifiers |
| M7 | Full-file overrides of on-actions, character interactions or scripted rules | Drops succession, trade, religion and DLC-gating changes, including possible game-over fixes | Re-merge changed entries; use separate on-action files for additions |
| M8 | References removed buildings, titles, effects, events or holy sites | Missing database objects; history or scripted branches fail | Search [§21](#21-complete-removed-key-reference) for the exact keys |

### 🟢 Low Risk

| # | What your mod does | What to review |
|---|---|---|
| L1 | Reuses vanilla localization or text icons | Building localization lost the `building_` prefix; many Development/opinion keys changed |
| L2 | Reuses vanilla textures, meshes or animations | 466 `gfx/` paths disappeared, some through moves/renames; check referenced paths against the target tag |
| L3 | Changes culture, traits, artifacts, innovations or men-at-arms balance | Existing keys often survive with different tax, control, needs or stationing effects |
| L4 | Uses the old `supported_version="1.20.*"` | Set it to `1.21.*` after validation |

## 3. Migration Checklist

- [ ] Compare every vanilla file your mod replaces with `base/1.21.0.0`, especially holdings, buildings,
      province history, governments, on-actions and GUI.
- [ ] Replace Development API names, modifier types, defines and localization with their Prosperity equivalents.
- [ ] Replace county opinion names; redesign monthly control-growth bonuses around baseline and convergence.
- [ ] Split tax bonuses by category where appropriate; distinguish province tax from character income.
- [ ] Add districts for custom buildings, including special and great buildings; update all custom holding lists.
- [ ] Move runtime building effects/checks into district scope. Update province history to the district format.
- [ ] Review removed keys in [§21](#21-complete-removed-key-reference), including buildings and nested landed titles.
- [ ] Update changed scripted-effect arguments and the law-approval rule.
- [ ] Configure custom governments' trade access, title-MAA setup, tax slot type, house power and title types.
- [ ] Review faith/rite restrictions, the new dietary/usury doctrines and non-DLC playability.
- [ ] For map mods, update province IDs, markets, resource potentials, roads and `spline_network.splnet` together.
- [ ] Rebase county, law, domicile and realm UI work; update custom building localization.
- [ ] Test a new game in each supported bookmark with `-debug_mode`; exercise succession, construction,
      trade and law changes, then save/reload and inspect `error.log`.
- [ ] Update the descriptor's supported version once those checks pass.

---

## 4. Development → Prosperity

This affects script, modifiers, innovations, council tasks, history, the UI and defines. Renaming a
reference fixes its spelling; it does not establish that an old Development bonus is balanced for Prosperity.

| 1.20 name / location | 1.21 equivalent |
|---|---|
| `development_level` | `prosperity_level` |
| `development_rate` | `prosperity_rate` |
| `development_towards_level_increase` | `prosperity_towards_level_increase` |
| `change_development_level` | `change_prosperity_level` |
| `change_development_progress` | `change_prosperity_progress` |
| `development_growth`, `development_growth_factor` | `prosperity_growth`, `prosperity_growth_factor` |
| `development_decline`, `development_decline_factor` | `prosperity_decline`, `prosperity_decline_factor` |
| `<terrain/region>_development_growth` and `_factor` | Corresponding `_prosperity_growth` and `_factor` names |
| Government flag `affected_by_development` | `affected_by_prosperity` |
| Geographical region `generate_modifiers` | `generate_prosperity_modifiers` |
| Map mode `county_development` | `county_prosperity` |
| `innovation_development_01` … `_04` | `innovation_prosperity_01` … `_04` |
| `diligent_development_focus_decision` | `diligent_prosperity_focus_decision` |
| `promote_other_culture_development_decision` | `promote_other_culture_prosperity_decision` |
| `unity_promote_development_decision` | `unity_promote_prosperity_decision` |

Many removed scripted values/effects have the same name with `prosperity` substituted. The complete
removed lists and mechanically matched replacements are in [§21](#21-complete-removed-key-reference).
Check individual bodies: farming, manufacturing, needs and settlement size now handle parts of the
economy that a generic Development increase used to stand in for.

Do not replace every occurrence of the English word “development” automatically. For example,
`treasury_vassal_development` still appears in the new government documentation.

## 5. Popular Opinion, Control & Settlement Size

### Popular opinion

| Old | New |
|---|---|
| `county_opinion` trigger | `popular_opinion` |
| `county_holder_opinion` trigger | `base_popular_opinion` |
| `county_opinion_add` | `popular_opinion_add` |
| `county_opinion_add_even_if_baron` | `popular_opinion_add_even_if_baron` |
| `different_faith_county_opinion_mult` and `_even_if_baron` | Corresponding `different_faith_popular_opinion_*` modifiers |
| `same_rite_county_opinion_add` | `same_rite_popular_opinion_add` |
| `same_faith_different_rite_county_opinion_add` | `same_faith_different_rite_popular_opinion_add` |

Popular opinion has its own monthly change and thresholds. `NCounty.POPULAR_OPINION_MIN/MAX` are
−100 / 100; the base monthly change is 0.5, with a minimum of 0.1. Existing faction, legitimacy,
religion, travel-danger and event calculations were updated to use it.

### Control

**Control now trends toward a baseline.** Old `monthly_county_control_growth_*` and
`monthly_county_control_decline_*` modifier definitions are removed. Audit each bonus by purpose:

- Use `control_baseline_add` to change the target level.
- Use `monthly_control_change` and the other new control-change modifiers to change convergence.
- Use `county_control_baseline` for the corresponding trigger calculation.

The default baseline is **35**, default starting control changes **100 → 25**, and control lost on
hostile ownership changes drops **75 → 20**. The old `NDomain.LEVIES_AT_ZERO_COUNTY_CONTROL` and
`TAX_AT_ZERO_COUNTY_CONTROL` constants are absent. Carrying over a “+0.2 control growth” modifier
without deciding which new behavior it represents will give the wrong result.

### Settlement size

Settlement size is a new province-level economic input. History uses `province_size`; modifiers include
`province_size_baseline_add`, `monthly_province_size_change` and `_mult`, and separate change modifiers
above/below baseline. There are also county-capital and character-capital variants.

The raw default baseline is **150**, size steps are **100**, maximum raw size is **1,000**, and maximum
named level is **10**. History `province_size = 3` sets level 3, or raw size 300. Do not confuse history
levels with raw modifier values. Size interacts with holding needs, control and Prosperity; vanilla
handles size-level changes in `common/on_action/county_on_actions.txt`.

---

## 6. Tax Categories & Income

There are eleven tax categories: **generic, agricultural, mining, religious, urban, artisan, maritime,
tribal, civic, woodland and commerce**. They have province tax modifiers and character income modifiers.

| Old use | New use |
|---|---|
| Holding/province `tax_mult` | `all_tax_mult` for all categories, or a category such as `generic_tax_mult` |
| `tax_per_piety_level` | `generic_tax_per_piety_level` |
| Province modifier `monthly_income` | `monthly_generic_tax`, or the appropriate `monthly_<category>_tax` |
| Province trigger `monthly_income` | `total_monthly_tax_income` |
| `monthly_income_per_piety_level` modifier definition | Removed; choose the appropriate supported category/level bonus |

For example, a province can receive `monthly_agricultural_tax`, while its holder's character modifiers
can use `monthly_agricultural_income` and `monthly_agricultural_income_mult`. `monthly_income` is still
a valid **character** modifier; replacing it everywhere would damage otherwise valid character effects.

Review tax changes in artifacts, traits, lifestyle perks, traditions, terrain, holy sites, council tasks,
tax jurisdictions and government bonuses, not only buildings. Needs can grant conditional tax output,
so a building's headline income is no longer necessarily its full income.

---

## 7. Holdings, Districts & Buildings

The hierarchy is now **province → holding → districts → buildings**. A district has a primary building
and can list additional building chains. The district owns the slot type; a building no longer declares
itself to be a special/duchy/great building through the old building `type` field.

| Old definition | New definition |
|---|---|
| Holding `primary_building = castle_01` | `primary_district = castle_holding_district` |
| Holding `buildings = { … }` | `districts = { … }` |
| Special building's `type` | A wrapping district with `type = special` |
| Great building repair fields | `rebuild_cost` / `can_rebuild` on its great district |
| Province-wide building slot bonuses | Review district slots separately from building slots within districts |

New building folders include `holding_buildings`, `holding_district_buildings`,
`regular_district_buildings`, `resource_district_buildings`, `great_district_buildings`,
`entrepot_district_buildings`, `trade_post_buildings` and `special_buildings`.
Seventeen old building `.txt` paths disappeared. Some definitions moved; **412 keys are actually absent**
from the active target building database, while 577 surviving keys have changed bodies.

A minimal custom regular district can follow this structure:

```text
# common/districts/my_districts.txt
my_workshop_district = {
    type = regular
    primary_building = my_workshop_1
}

# common/buildings/my_buildings.txt
my_workshop_1 = {
    construction_time = 360
    cost = { gold = 100 }
    province_modifier = { monthly_artisan_tax = 0.2 }
}
```

Also list `my_workshop_district` in each holding type that should offer it and add localization for the
district, `my_workshop_1` and `my_workshop_1_desc`. Additional building chains go in the district's
`buildings` list. `district_level_requirement` gates buildings by the level of their parent district.

Runtime changes need more than key substitution:

| Old operation | New operation / scope |
|---|---|
| `add_building`, `remove_building` in province scope | Operate on the selected district |
| Province `has_free_building_slot` | `has_free_district_slot` for district slots; building-slot checks belong to a district |
| `add_special_building`, `add_special_building_slot` | `add_special_district`, `add_special_district_slot` |
| `add_great_building`, `set_great_building` and slot variants | Corresponding `*_great_district` operations |
| `ruin_great_building`, `rebuild_great_building` | `ruin_great_district`, `rebuild_great_district` |
| `has_special_building`, `has_great_building` and slot checks | Corresponding `*_district` checks |
| `has_ruined_great_building` | `has_ruined_great_district` |
| `has_ongoing_construction` | Choose `has_ongoing_holding_construction` or `has_ongoing_trade_post_construction` |
| Scripted list `special_building_province` | `special_district_province` |

**Scope exception:** construction callbacks such as `on_building_completed` still document the
**province** as root, with `scope:character` for the payer when available. Do not change an entire
on-action into district scope merely because its building operations changed.

The new building documentation also describes `trade_good_need`, conditional modifier collections,
`fallback`, `override_modifiers`, market modifiers and more conditional province/character modifiers.
Use the new `_buildings.info` and `_districts.info`; `_old_buildings.info` is a legacy reference and
does not restore removed definitions.

---

## 8. Province, Character & Title History

### Province history

Use a district block for buildings. For example, using existing 1.21 keys:

```text
holding = castle_holding
province_size = 3
districts = {
    district = {
        type = castle_holding_district
        level = 3
        buildings = { castle_garrison_1 }
    }
    district = {
        type = guild_district
        level = 3
        buildings = { agricultural_guilds_5 }
    }
}
```

- District history needs an explicit holding; `holding = auto` does not support this setup.
- The holding district must match the holding's `primary_district`. A mismatch is skipped with an error.
- A later `districts` entry replaces previous regular districts and their buildings. The holding district
  is retained if omitted; explicitly scripting it replaces its extra buildings and defaults its level to 1.
- `special_district_slot` enables a special slot; use `districts` to construct the district itself.
- `entrepot_district_slot` enables an entrepôt slot.
- `trade_post = { type = … owner = <character ID> level = … buildings = { … } }` creates a trade post.
  Its owner must be alive at the selected start date and able to own trade posts.
- Province-history `effect` executes after holding/regular-building setup and before the documented
  special/duchy-capital stage. Review effects that depend on setup order.

### Characters and titles

Character history documents `create_betrothal`, `create_betrothal_matrilineal` and `break_betrothal`.
It also clarifies that direct `set_house`, `set_culture` and `set_character_faith_no_effect` entries are
errors: use `dynasty_house`, `culture` and `faith` for history fields. This is a documentation correction,
not proof that every newly documented field was introduced in 1.21.

History changes include 106 added files, 10 removed files and 590 modified files. The landed-title scan,
including nested titles, finds **2,185 added keys and 173 removed keys**. Removed keys include ordinary
baronies/counties and many noble-family titles. Check exact references in [§21](#21-complete-removed-key-reference);
do not assume that an old family title's tier or key survived.

---

## 9. Trade Goods, Markets, Routes & Needs

| Database | Launch definitions | Purpose |
|---|---:|---|
| `common/trade/trade_good_types` | 44 | Goods, base prices, basic-good flag, optional origin |
| `common/trade/trade_good_categories` | 41 | Need categories, grouping categories and expertise tracks |
| `common/trade/county_potential_trade_goods` | 35 | Counties in which particular goods can be produced |
| `common/trade/trade_market_toll_levels` | 6 | Market toll levels |
| `common/trade_tasks` | 3 | Trader tasks |
| `common/trade_goods_graphics` | 69 | Trade-good graphical definitions |

A market is initialized from a de jure duchy. A duchy's `market_capital = b_...` can select a barony
with a holding in that duchy's de jure counties after history runs; otherwise its capital county's
capital barony is used. This applies to all bookmarks, while existing saves keep their existing markets.
Both road endpoints must be market capitals, so moving a market also requires updating its roads.

A new good needs production as well as a definition. Add it to the appropriate category and county
potentials, grant production through buildings/modifiers, and provide localization and graphics. A good
can belong to **at most one need category** (`need = yes`) and additional ordinary categories.

Goods and categories generate production, buy-price and sell-price modifiers, such as
`trade_good_silk_production_add` and `trade_good_silk_sell_price_mult`. Category expertise can define
`levels`, `per_level_interval` and `milestone_interval`.

Buildings consume goods through `trade_good_need`, specifying either `trade_good` or
`trade_good_category`, an `amount`, and granted `modifiers`. Conditional need fields include culture,
rite, government, situation, dynasty perk, province size, terrain and coastal/riverside requirements.
Do not assume an unmet need automatically applies a penalty: inspect the building's granted and fallback effects.

Governments choose **profit** or **distribution** routes. Distribution routes supply buildings within a
realm; profit routes use trade capacity, competitiveness and buy/sell prices. They are separate permissions
in government flags. Trade scripts use market, route, good/category and trader scopes; check the relevant
on-action comments before accessing saved scopes.

Launch values in `NTrade` include capacity **35**, base maximum route length **10**, base profit-route
count **1**, base market count **3**, profit-route goods cap **6**, and base trade-post limit **2**.
These are release-file values; development diary numbers can differ.

---

## 10. Roads & Map Changes

Road definitions live in `common/roads/road_types`; tier modifiers live in
`common/roads/road_modifiers`. The launch files define **4,201 road entries**. Roads list province IDs,
their initial tier and `spline_asset_id`; vanilla uses 0 for land and 1 for sea. Tier 0 is not drawn.
Road modifiers can affect provinces, trade routes and travellers.

**Custom maps must supply their road data.** Define connections between market capitals and supply
matching spline geometry at `gfx/map/spline_network/spline_network.splnet`.

1. In debug mode, `SplineNetwork.AppendAssets` generates missing splines. Editing a road-definition file
   while the game runs also reloads roads and generates missing geometry.
2. For a total conversion, the developer diary describes `SplineNetwork.ClearAssets confirm` before
   generating, to avoid retaining vanilla geometry for reused province IDs.
3. Tune geometry in the map editor and move the generated network into the mod at the same relative path.
4. Remove the generated copy from `Documents/Paradox Interactive/Crusader Kings III/gfx/map/spline_network/`
   once it is packaged. That user-directory copy overrides game/mod geometry across updates.

The `.splnet` is a single binary artifact; independently modified networks need a combined replacement.
Road styles and moving caravans/ships are under `gfx/map/spline_network/spline_styles`, `spline_types`
and `trade_route_entities`. Province/county movement speed modifiers also exist independently of roads.

The map itself is **not unchanged**, unlike the previous post-launch report:

- `definition.csv`, `adjacencies.csv`, `default.map`, `climate.txt` and geographical-region files changed.
- `provinces.png`, `rivers.png`, heightmap images/data and `nodes.dat` changed in the repository metadata.
- `default.map` adds sea provinces **13312–13317** for the Somali–Laccadive connection and river provinces
  **13439–13443** / **13444–13445** for the Weser / Oder.
- `map_data/geographical_regions/entrepot_regions.txt` is new.
- Regions support `province_modifier`, `county_modifier` and `generate_prosperity_modifiers`.
- `common/province_terrain/01_province_properties.txt` receives extensive province setup changes.

Binary entries in this repository are placeholders with file metadata; changed placeholders establish
that the upstream asset changed, not that this checkout contains usable replacement image data.

---

## 11. Governments, Domiciles & Title Types

`merchant_government` is new. `republic_government` survives under its existing key but gains assembly,
trade, noble-family and title-MAA behavior. `merchant_company` is a new domicile type, with extensive
buildings under `common/domiciles/buildings/00_merchant_company_buildings.txt`.

Review these fields in custom governments:

| Field / behavior | Migration detail |
|---|---|
| `affected_by_prosperity` | Replaces the old Development flag |
| `profit_trade_routes`, `distribution_trade_routes` | Explicitly choose access; the documented distribution default is yes, subject to eligibility checks |
| `uses_assembly` | Enables assembly involvement in laws/decisions; requires the corresponding script setup |
| `administrative_title_maa_setup` → `title_maa_setup` | Rename the old field used by vanilla Celestial government; explicitly configure custom administrative governments |
| `title_maa_setup` modes | No default; old documented `main_administrative_tier_and_top_liege` becomes `main_tier_and_top_liege`; other modes include `vassals_and_top_liege`, `top_vassals_and_top_liege`, `grand_mayor` |
| `minimum_provincial_maa_tier` | No default in the new documentation; vanilla sets it explicitly for relevant governments |
| `tax_slot_type` | Selects the government's tax-slot type |
| `house_power` | A script value evaluated for the house; review old global house-power overrides |
| `max_dread` | Optional government-specific maximum |
| `build_queue_reserve_categories` | Which currencies may be reserved in the build queue |
| `realm_mask_offset`, `realm_mask_scale` | Government coat-of-arms mask placement |

`title_types` is a new database with 11 definitions for regular titles, republics, merchant/noble
families, holy orders, mercenary companies, ecclesiastical/cardinal titles, adventurers and nomads.
It centralizes title defaults, prefixes, domicile type, landless behavior, house-title assignment and
trade-post ownership. Prefixes help classify static titles and older-save titles; custom family/title
systems should review this instead of copying only vanilla flags.

Playability rules now distinguish `playable_republics`, `landless_adventurer` and `roads_to_power`
feature checks. Preserve the new gating when overriding `is_character_allowed_to_be_player`.

The updated government documentation also replaces an obsolete `vassal_contract` example with
`vassal_contract_group`. Vanilla already used `vassal_contract_group` in 1.20.0.2; this is a documentation
correction rather than a newly introduced migration requirement.

---

## 12. Laws, Assemblies & Confederations

There are **19 new law groups and 82 new law definitions**. They include merchant company size/purpose,
republic type, army organization, assembly composition/authority/voting thresholds, terms, senate size,
trade policy, title tier and permissions for executions, banishment, revocations and wars.

- Scripted rule `approves_of_succession_law_change` becomes **`approves_of_law_change`**.
- Scripted trigger `opposes_succession_law_change_trigger` becomes **`opposes_law_change_trigger`**.
- Law fields include `ai_approval`, `term_length`, `allow_same_successor`, `take_realm_capital`,
  `manual_law_change` and `voting_session`; law groups document `can_propose`.
- `term_length` is in **months**, evaluated in title scope. Missing/zero means a life appointment.
- Assembly voting and term-end callbacks are listed in [§16](#16-on-actions).
- The generic change-law UI replaces the old succession-specific window.

Confederations now use **`confederation_base = character / house / title`**, replacing
`house_based_confederation`. `trade_league` and `lombard_league` are added. Membership assumptions must
match the selected base; a trade league can have titles as members.

House/member modifier blocks are now documented **inside cohesion levels**. New title-based modifier
collections distinguish leading/member holders, noble families and landed vassals. Review
`can_receive_modifiers_from_confederation`, `member_would_join_war`, parameters and
`diplomatic_icon_frame_index`. The new `confederation_aspirations` database supplies seven bloc aspirations.

---

## 13. Religion & The Intervening 1.20 Patches

The Faith → Rite structure remains, but religion data and the economy now interact:

- `old_bon` is added as a Faith key; no existing Rite keys are removed in this comparison.
- `precepts` is a new doctrine category. New groups are `ahimsa_doctrine`, `alcohol_restrictions`,
  `dietary_restrictions`, `doctrine_usury` and `is_yazidi_faith`.
- Sixteen doctrine definitions cover alcohol, diet, usury and special restrictions. These influence trade
  and building needs, so custom rites should review their parameter setup.
- The 53 unused holy sites removed in 1.20.1 remain absent. Restoring all of them can reintroduce the
  blocked-barony issue for dynamic holy sites. Their complete key list is in [§21](#21-complete-removed-key-reference).

For a mod coming directly from 1.20.0.2, also incorporate these intervening fixes/hooks:

- `can_change_faith` is checked for faith changes, including script. Its new body uses
  `is_proto_hof_trigger`; inspect that rule before scripting changes to foundational Church heads.
- `seek_indulgences_has_capital_archbishop_trigger` and
  `seek_indulgences_landed_clergy_recipient_trigger` were removed. Use the new actor-capital-archbishop
  check / current recipient trigger logic.
- Reformation has `special_interaction = reform_faith` and `convert_on_reform_interaction`.
- The intervening 1.20.1 modding notes add doctrine-scope `doctrine_divergence` and
  `doctrine_in_same_group` triggers.
- Tenets support `historical_glossary`; rites can be history-only with `history = yes`.
- Domicile construction gained sound hooks. `PlayBuildingNotificationSfx` was replaced by
  `PlayBuildingSfx` / `PlayConstructionSfx`.
- Church, cardinal and Ecumenical Council UI and scripts received fixes; many 1.20.0.2 overrides discard them.
- 1.20.1.1 primarily updates non-English localization. 1.20.1.2 additionally fixes portrait crashes,
  holy-site visibility, monastic-order bestowal, religious inspirations and adventurer estate succession.

The [existing post-launch report](README.md#crusader-kings-iii--12002--12011-crozier) explains the
1.20.1.1 subset. It is a historical comparison; use this report's 1.21 definitions for the final migration.

---

## 14. Script API Changes & New Engine Features

### Existing names with changed ownership or semantics

| Name | What to do |
|---|---|
| `is_governor` | Removed as a scripted trigger because the check is now engine-backed; do **not** remove valid calls merely because the script definition disappeared |
| `governor_efficiency` | Removed as a scripted value; engine-backed calculation now uses efficiency defines and `governor_efficiency_add` / `_mult` |
| `house_power` trigger | Use `house_power_score`; the old scripted value named `house_power_score` is separately removed as house-power setup moves into governments |
| Building effects / free-slot triggers | Review scope as well as name; see [§7](#7-holdings-districts--buildings) |
| Law approval and confederation fields | Use the generalized law rule and `confederation_base` |

The removed-effect/trigger/value lists in [§21](#21-complete-removed-key-reference) are **script database
differences**, not a complete list of removed engine commands. Most removed building helpers have been
replaced by district/resource-specific logic. Compare the new implementation instead of restoring old helpers wholesale.

### New general modding capabilities

The official modding diary describes these engine changes. This repository has no generated engine
`script_docs` dump, so check current in-game documentation for exact argument schemas before using them:

- **Variable maps:** scope/global key–value storage in addition to variables and lists.
- **Scoped database arguments:** dynamic domicile-title creation can take a government-type scope;
  aptitude comparisons can take court-position-type scopes; war effects/checks can take CB scopes.
- **Men-at-arms-type scopes:** support scoped MAA types in regiment creation / army spawning.
- **Modifier queries:** `modifier_value` compares accumulated character modifiers; the diary also
  announces a corresponding GUI getter. Verify the getter's spelling in an engine dump before using it.
- **Province/county movement speed modifiers**, independently of road travel bonuses.

Shipped `.info` files and vanilla usage also show `not_parameter` on conditional culture/rite modifiers,
`ai_frequency_by_tier_government_override` on interactions, region modifiers, rival situation participant
groups, automatic house-aspiration levels, title-based confederations, and four new decision selectors
for buildings, trade markets, routes and good categories.

---

## 15. Changed Scripted-Effect Parameters

These are the complete changed `$PARAM$` sets found in existing scripted effects. No corresponding
signature changes were found in existing scripted triggers, script values or scripted modifiers.

| Effect | 1.20.0.2 | 1.21 | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| `purchase_land_interaction_effect` | No substitutions | `TITLE_GIVER`, `TITLE_RECEIVER` | `07_dlc_ep3_scripted_effects.txt` |
| `purchase_land_interaction_shortened_effect` | No substitutions | `TITLE_GIVER`, `TITLE_RECEIVER` | Same file |
| `ep3_become_landed_transfer_effect` | `ENNOBLED_ADVENTURER`, `REASON`, `TITLE_LIST`, `TITLE_RECEIVER`, `TYPE` | `NEVER_ENNOBLED`, `REASON`, `TITLE_LIST`, `TITLE_RECEIVER`, `TYPE` | Same file |
| `pam_antipope_removed_catalyst_effect` | No substitutions | `FAITH` | `pam_antipope_effects.txt` |
| `pam_offer_great_schism_stance_effect` | No substitutions | `DELAYED` | `pam_effects.txt` |

The purchase effects explicitly save the passed receiver as purchaser and giver as seller. For example,
use the appropriate actor/recipient scopes for your interaction:

```text
purchase_land_interaction_effect = {
    TITLE_GIVER = scope:recipient
    TITLE_RECEIVER = scope:actor
}

pam_antipope_removed_catalyst_effect = { FAITH = scope:defender.faith }
pam_offer_great_schism_stance_effect = { DELAYED = yes }
```

For `ep3_become_landed_transfer_effect`, do not merely swap the parameter label while assuming the old
meaning. The body compares `$NEVER_ENNOBLED$` with `flag:no`; review the call's intended ennoblement behavior.
`DELAYED = yes` is used during title gain so AI does not choose a Schism stance for a character about to
become player-controlled; the religion-change path uses `no`.

---

## 16. On-Actions

The script inventory gains **134 on-action keys**, loses **3** and changes **174** surviving bodies.
Some additions are script-only helper pulses; their presence does not mean the engine fires them itself.

| New / renamed hook family | Notes |
|---|---|
| `on_great_district_rebuilt` | Replaces `on_great_building_rebuilt` |
| `on_province_size_level_increased`, `on_province_size_level_decreased` | Settlement-size changes |
| `on_trade_route_created`, `on_trade_route_edited`, `on_trade_route_destroyed` | Route lifecycle; root is owner for the documented create/edit callbacks |
| `on_trade_good_category_level_up`, `on_trade_good_category_level_down` | Expertise changes |
| `on_trader_assigned` | Trader assignment |
| `on_trade_monopoly_invalidated`, `on_toll_exception_changed`, `on_toll_exception_ended` | Trade rights |
| `on_assembly_vote_started`, `on_assembly_vote_ended`, `on_term_ended` | Republican government lifecycle |
| `on_betrothal` | New vanilla callback entry |
| `on_gdp_trader_arrived_at_market`, `on_market_changed_toll_level` | Explicitly documented as scripted callbacks |

The create/edit route hooks can run for **tooltip generation with a null `scope:trade_route`**. Guard
route access with `exists`; `scope:trader` can also be absent. This matters for custom UI/summary effects.

`stress_pulse` and `silk_road_market_visit_travel_planner_cancel_on_action` disappear as script definitions.
Revisit any mod callbacks attached to them. The on-action documentation also corrects examples to
`random_on_action` and `yearly_playable_pulse` and documents `fallback_event`; these documentation edits
alone do not establish that those spellings are new engine changes.

---

## 17. Culture, Traits, Warfare & Other Content

- **Cultures:** 11 added keys: `arpitan`, `geatish`, `icelandic`, `italo_norman`, `mandaean`, `mustaarabi`,
  `norse_gael`, `novgorodian`, `parsi`, `remenkimi`, `serbi`. No existing culture keys are removed.
- **Innovations:** 29 added, 4 Development innovations removed. New production/craft technologies include
  agronomy, heavy plow, deep mining, glassmaking, monasticism and merchant law. Existing innovations and
  traditions were rebalanced for resource districts, needs and Prosperity.
- **Traits:** 5 added: `adopted_no_inheritance`, `dealbreaker`, `education_republican_knowledge_5`,
  `lifestyle_merchant`, `merchant_associate`. No old trait definitions are removed; 123 bodies changed.
- **Men-at-arms:** `condottieri` is added; 110 surviving definitions changed. Recheck prices, maintenance,
  recruitment and stationing support instead of assuming old economy-based balance still works.
- **Schemes:** 57 scheme types added, including trade opportunities, patron contracts and republican
  political schemes. Agent types, critical moments and existing schemes also changed.
- **Decisions/interactions:** 90 decision definitions added, 4 removed; 94 interaction definitions added,
  none removed. Many existing definitions changed even though their keys survived.
- **Activities/situations:** `activity_carnival` and a Guelph–Ghibelline situation are added, with events,
  catalysts, rival participant groups and dedicated UI.
- **Relations:** creditor/debtor and additional puppet relation definitions are added.
- **Great Projects/artifacts/dynasty content:** republic projects, merchant/company artifacts and new
  legacy content accompany large changes to existing economy and religious modifiers.

Counts include debug/test definitions where shipped. Use the database index to find your mod's affected
systems; a surviving name is not evidence that its effects, permissions or scopes are unchanged.

---

## 18. GUI, Data Binding, Localization & Graphics

The sole removed `.gui` file is `window_succession_change_law.gui`. New files cover assemblies, voting
results, generic law changes, decision approval, build queues, republics, trade/markets/goods/routes,
landless-ruler filters, trade-needs widgets and new decision/event widgets.

The county view alone changes by 10,669 added/deleted lines. Other priority views are `hud.gui`,
`window_title.gui`, `window_my_realm.gui`, `window_domicile.gui`, `window_government_administration.gui`,
`window_dynasty_house.gui`, `window_military.gui`, `window_silk_road.gui` and shared map modes/tooltips.

`my_realm_coa_tiny_item`, `widget_building_item_ledger`

Two data-binding files are added and two modified. Review merchant macros in `gdp_data_bindings.txt`,
and the changes to existing general/religion macros. **A data function disappearing from vanilla's usage
is not proof that the engine removed it.** Check the new implementation and an engine dump when updating custom UI.

### Localization

Building localization changes from **`building_<key>` / `building_<key>_desc`** to
**`<key>` / `<key>_desc`**. Districts have their own names and descriptions. This is particularly easy
to miss when a custom building definition loads correctly but displays a raw localization key.

Across English localization, 35,849 unique keys are added and 2,204 disappear. These are inventory
differences, not 2,204 confirmed independent feature removals; many are renamed building/economy labels.
The removed English key list is included in [§21](#21-complete-removed-key-reference).

Across all languages, 918 localization files are added, 18 removed and 4,276 modified. Mods supporting
non-English text should also merge the 1.20 localization hotfixes rather than copying only English changes.

### Graphics and portraits

`gfx/` has 3,210 added, 466 removed and 855 modified paths, including path moves represented as an
addition/removal. Changes cover district/trade UI, roads, settlement sprawl, new merchant/republic portrait
assets and existing graphics/shaders. The new `holding_sprawl_graphics` and `holding_sprawl_patterns`
databases define settlement presentation; trade-good visuals also have a dedicated database.

For an exact asset-path audit use the file-status commands in [§23](#23-validation--reproducible-diffs).
This report does not infer a replacement texture merely from a similar filename.

---

## 19. Defines & Balance Changes

The define scan across all define files finds **202 added, 18 removed and 17 changed values**. It compares
complete values, including arrays, after stripping comments/whitespace. New namespaces include `NLaw`,
`NTrade` and `NRoad`; existing county/province/administrative settings gain substantial new controls.

| Setting | 1.20.0.2 | 1.21.0 |
|---|---|---|
| `NCounty.DEFAULT_COUNTY_CONTROL` | 100 | 25 |
| `NCounty.COUNTY_CONTROL_LOSS_ON_HOSTILE_OWNER_CHANGE` | 75 | 20 |
| Control baseline | No corresponding new-model define | `COUNTY_CONTROL_DEFAULT_BASELINE = 35` |
| `NCulture.MINIMUM_INNOVATIONS_TO_NEXT_ERA` | 8 | 10 |
| `NCulture.INNOVATION_PROGRESS_CHANCE_SPREAD_AND_FASCINATION_MULTIPLIER` | 2 | 1.2 |
| `NProvince.BARONY_BUILDING_SLOTS` / `COUNTY_BUILDING_SLOTS` | 2 / 3 | 1 / 1, interpreted within districts |
| `NProvince.BARONY_DISTRICT_SLOTS` / `COUNTY_DISTRICT_SLOTS` | Absent | 1 / 1 |
| Maximum Development/Prosperity tax bonus | `TAX_AT_MAX_COUNTY_DEVELOPMENT = 0.5` | `TAX_AT_MAX_COUNTY_PROSPERITY = 0.25` |
| `NRaid.MONTHS_OF_RAID_LOOT` / `LESS_MONTHS_OF_RAID_LOOT` | 12 / 6 | 10 / 4 |
| `NTravel.PROVINCE_OWNER_OPINION_DANGER_MULTIPLIER` | 0.5 | 0.25 |
| County opinion danger multiplier | `COUNTY_OPINION_DANGER_MULTIPLIER = 0.5` | `POPULAR_OPINION_DANGER_MULTIPLIER = 0.25` |
| Concurrent mercenary baseline | No new-model limit define | `NMercenary.MERCENARY_BASE_LIMIT = 1` |

Other additions include law-vote durations/approval thresholds, initial term limits, merchant Acumen
thresholds, governor-efficiency skill weights and bounds, trade prices/capacity/route limits, toll cooldowns,
road construction/decay timing, settlement-size levels, and UI/graphics paths. The appendix lists all define
changes so custom define overrides can be reviewed without relying on highlights.

## 22. Database Change Index

Every changed `common/` database group is included below. Counts compare top-level keys across
active `.txt` files, except the landed-title row, which includes nested titles. “Changed body” includes
comment changes and ignores whitespace; it is a review count, not a count of semantic engine changes.
Folder groups such as `coat_of_arms`, `great_projects` and `schemes` combine their relevant files.

## 23. Validation & Reproducible Diffs

Fetch the immutable launch-version tag if necessary. These commands compare snapshots without updating
the working tree or merging the game update:

```bash
git fetch --no-tags upstream refs/tags/base/1.21.0.0:refs/tags/base/1.21.0.0

# Complete path inventory, including assets; moves appear as delete + add.
git diff --no-renames --name-status base/1.20.0.2 base/1.21.0.0 -- base/game
git diff --no-renames --stat base/1.20.0.2 base/1.21.0.0 -- base/game

# Review a specific vanilla file your mod overrides.
git diff base/1.20.0.2 base/1.21.0.0 -- base/game/common/holdings/00_holdings.txt

# Separate the new expansion from the last pre-1.21 hotfix.
git diff base/1.20.1.2 base/1.21.0.0 -- base/game/common

# Read target documentation even while your working tree is still on 1.20.
git show base/1.21.0.0:base/game/common/districts/_districts.info
git show base/1.21.0.0:base/game/common/buildings/_buildings.info
git show base/1.21.0.0:base/game/history/_provinces.info
```

Replace `upstream` with the remote for `jesec/ck3-mod-base` if yours has another name.

Search your mod for migration candidates, then review each match in context:

```bash
rg -n 'development_|change_development|county_opinion|county_holder_opinion' mod
rg -n 'monthly_county_control_(growth|decline)|\btax_mult\b|tax_per_piety_level' mod
rg -n 'add_building|remove_building|has_free_building_slot|has_ongoing_construction' mod
rg -n '(special|great)_building|house_based_confederation|approves_of_succession_law_change' mod
rg -n 'purchase_land_interaction(_shortened)?_effect|ep3_become_landed_transfer_effect' mod
rg -n 'pam_antipope_removed_catalyst_effect|pam_offer_great_schism_stance_effect' mod
rg -n 'PlayBuildingNotificationSfx|window_succession_change_law|county_development' mod
```

Search any separate mod source directory and modified `base/game` files too; this repository's sample
`mod/` folder is not a substitute for auditing your actual overrides.

Start a **new** game with `-debug_mode`. Inspect
`Documents/Paradox Interactive/Crusader Kings III/logs/error.log` for unknown effect/trigger names,
invalid database links, wrong scope types, missing GUI types/data functions, mismatched holding districts,
and `Could not find spline network strip`.

Exercise at least construction and upgrades, special/great districts, size/control changes, law votes,
succession, conversion, trade routes and save/reload. Test DLC-enabled and non-DLC paths your mod supports.
Only then test an old save separately; loading successfully does not establish economic or succession correctness.

---

## 24. Methodology & Sources

- **Baseline:** `base/1.20.0.2`, commit `9c97d375a`; target:
  **`base/1.21.0.0`**, commit `720e3b1ed2749f47a98468e39350a3e2aea7ceb7`.
  The comparison includes every intervening 1.20 patch.
- **File inventory:** compared Git blob IDs for every `base/game` path. No rename detection; a moved
  file is an addition and removal. This makes the totals independent of Git's rename-limit heuristics.
- **Definition inventory:** parsed top-level assignments from active `.txt` files, accounting for braces,
  quoted strings and comments. Grouped buildings across their reorganized subfolders; compared keys by
  database rather than by filename. Nested landed-title keys were scanned separately. Inline helper
  effects/triggers in event files are listed separately from event IDs. Definition-body comparisons ignore
  whitespace; comments can still contribute to the “changed body” counts.
- **Parameter inventory:** compared `$PARAM$` sets in surviving scripted effect, trigger, value and modifier
  bodies. This does not detect every semantic or saved-scope change.
- **GUI/localization:** extracted type/template declarations and unique English localization keys.
  GUI call-use differences were treated as review clues, not authoritative engine API removals.
- **Defines:** compared individual namespace/key assignments across all define files, combining repeated
  namespaces and normalizing comments/whitespace in values, including multiline arrays.
- **Limits:** no generated engine `script_docs` dump or 1.21 mod runtime validation. Binary placeholders
  establish changed upstream files but cannot demonstrate visual correctness. This is a modding migration
  report, not a claim to reproduce every gameplay changelog item or every engine-internal change.

Primary references:

- [Pinned 1.21 source tree](https://github.com/jesec/ck3-mod-base/tree/720e3b1ed2749f47a98468e39350a3e2aea7ceb7/base/game)
  and [full version comparison](https://github.com/jesec/ck3-mod-base/compare/base/1.20.0.2...base/1.21.0.0).
